   with

```markdown
- Hub and arms (S8.5): the radial lobes, the branching mall, the care hub, and the foyer with halls share `HubArmsLayout` (internal, `Lod.Generators/HubArms/`): a square hub with up to four arms of one width, centred on its sides in the order east, north, west, south; a family with *n* arms uses the first *n* sides (two arms east and north, three a T open to plan south, four a cross), and the bounding box's south-west corner is at the origin. Each arm has its own frame, along from the hub and across from its clockwise side, in which the families cut their zones. An arm must not be wider than the hub, and at most four arms fit the axis-aligned layout (D-100); both are `InvalidParameter`. Arms narrower than the hub leave its corners as short outdoor walls, which get no window below 1 m (`WindowOmitted`). Zones are listed hub first, then arm by arm.
- Radial lobes (`SYN-TYP-010`, S8.5): `LobeCount` (3–4, default 4) lobes, `LobeLength` 10 m and `LobeWidth` 12 m, around a `CoreWidth` 12 m core `ST` (*Core*, a `Stair` zone); each lobe holds two dwellings side by side, `L{i}-U1` (the half clockwise of the lobe's axis) and `L{i}-U2`, both adjoining the core. With three lobes the core's south side is a façade. 624 m², 16 windows.
- Branching mall (`SYN-TYP-014`, S8.5): `BranchCount` (3–4, default 3) branches from a central court as wide as the branches, `2 · ShopDepth + MallWidth` = 32 m; each branch has a `BranchLength` 40 m shop wing on either side of an 8 m mall strip, `B{i}-S1`, `B{i}-S2` (`Retail`), and an `AnchorDepth` 30 m anchor `B{i}-AN` (`Retail`) across its end. The court and every strip are one zone `MALL` (`Mall`), which every shop wing and anchor adjoins. Floor height 5 m; 7744 m², 16 windows; with four branches the mall has no outdoor wall.
- Care hub (`SYN-TYP-018`, S8.5): `WingCount` (2–4, default 3) wings of `WingLength` 30 m around a `HubWidth` 16 m communal hub `HUB` (`CareCommunal`); each wing is a `CorridorWidth` 2.4 m corridor `W{i}-CO` (`Corridor`) from the hub to the wing's end between two bedroom zones `W{i}-B1`, `W{i}-B2` (`CareBedroom`) of `BedroomDepth` 6 m. The wing width `2 · BedroomDepth + CorridorWidth` must not exceed `HubWidth`. 1552 m², 16 windows; the six 0.8 m hub corners get none (`WindowOmitted`).
- Foyer with halls (`SYN-TYP-019`, S8.5): a `FoyerWidth` 20 m foyer `FO` (`Lobby`) with `HallCount` (2–3, default 2) activity halls `H1…Hn` (`ActivityHall`) of `HallLength` 30 m × `HallWidth` 20 m and a `SupportLength` 10 m support wing `SV` (`Service`): hall 1 east, the support wing north, hall 2 west, hall 3 south; zones `FO`, `H1…Hn`, `SV`. With two halls the foyer's south side is its entrance façade. Floor height 6 m; 1800 m², 10 windows.
- Non-residential families place one zone per department (D-097, [ADR-014](../decisions/ADR-014-program-types-and-department-zoning.md)); their default dimensions are controller design defaults (D-098). The remaining families of the four-source set are specified in ADR-006 and built in S8.6–S8.7.
```

6. Replace

```markdown
- *Semantic Merge* merges connected zones of the same space type; each group's footprint is the union of its zones as tiles (`PolygonOps.UnionTiles`), which keeps the zones' own vertices and may have holes: the four dwellings of the point plate become one ring around the core (ADR-002). *Perimeter Core* makes one perimeter zone per orientation plus a core, with corners split on the bisectors (ADR-008), also at re-entrant corners; an orientation with several separate façade runs, such as the open court's three south façades, gives the zones `P-South-1…`. A footprint with holes is offset on every ring: the court façades get their own zones `P-Court-North`, `P-Court-East`, `P-Court-South`, `P-Court-West`, named by the direction they face and listed after the outer zones, and the core is a ring around the court (ADR-008, S8.3). Every wing or other part of the footprint, including the wings between the outer and the court façades, must be wider than twice the depth, or the simplifier fails with `PlanTooNarrow`, whose message says so. *Single Zone per Floor* makes the floor one zone.
```

   with

```markdown
- *Semantic Merge* merges connected zones of the same space type; each group's footprint is the union of its zones as tiles (`PolygonOps.UnionTiles`), which keeps the zones' own vertices and may have holes: the four dwellings of the point plate become one ring around the core (ADR-002). *Perimeter Core* makes one perimeter zone per orientation plus a core, with corners split on the bisectors (ADR-008), also at re-entrant corners; an orientation with several separate façade runs, such as the open court's three south façades, gives the zones `P-South-1…`. A footprint with holes is offset on every ring: the court façades get their own zones `P-Court-North`, `P-Court-East`, `P-Court-South`, `P-Court-West`, named by the direction they face and listed after the outer zones, and the core is a ring around the court (ADR-008, S8.3). The perimeter zones keep the footprint's own vertices, not vertices rounded to the clipping grid, so a façade edge shorter than about 1 m, such as a hub corner of the care hub, is covered by the single-zone aggregators on a rotated plan (ADR-008, S8.5). Every wing or other part of the footprint, including the wings between the outer and the court façades, must be wider than twice the depth, or the simplifier fails with `PlanTooNarrow`, whose message says so. *Single Zone per Floor* makes the floor one zone.
```

8. Replace

```markdown
- **Preview Location (D-062).** The eleven plan generators, *Transform Plan*, the four plan simplifiers, and the four floor aggregators have an optional last input *Preview Location* (PL): a vector in model units, default `0,0,0` (a point converts to a vector). It moves only that component's viewport preview: the plan, floor, or building data, *Inspect*, *Validate*, and *Convert2BEM* keep the true coordinates, and downstream components do not inherit the move (each wraps its output with its own input). *Convert2BEM* has no such input.
```

It moves only that component's viewport preview: the plan, floor, or building data, *Inspect*, *Validate*, and *Convert2BEM* keep the true coordinates, and downstream components do not inherit the move (each wraps its output with its own input). *Convert2BEM* has no such input.

3. Replace

```markdown
`HubArmsLayout` (in `Lod.Generators/HubArms/`, internal) lays out a square hub of side `H` and up to four rectangular arms on its sides, in the order east, north, west, south (counter-clockwise from plan east), each arm centred on its side, of length `Lᵢ` away from the hub and width `A` along the side. An arm must not be wider than the hub (`A ≤ H`; a wider arm would overlap its neighbour beyond the hub's corner), which every family validates. The footprint is the union of hub and arms (a T for three arms, a cross for four), placed with the south-west corner of its bounding box at the origin. A hub side without an arm, and the hub's corners when `A < H`, are outdoor walls. The families subdivide the hub and the arms into their zones; zones are listed hub first, then arm by arm in the order above.
```

   with

```markdown
`HubArmsLayout` (in `Lod.Generators/HubArms/`, internal) lays out a square hub of side `H` and up to four rectangular arms on its sides, in the order east, north, west, south (counter-clockwise from plan east), each arm centred on its side, of length `Lᵢ` away from the hub and width `A` along the side. An arm must not be wider than the hub (`A ≤ H`; a wider arm would overlap its neighbour beyond the hub's corner), which every family validates. The footprint is the union of hub and arms (a T for three arms, a cross for four), placed with the south-west corner of its bounding box at the origin. A hub side without an arm, and the hub's corners when `A < H`, are outdoor walls. The families subdivide the hub and the arms into their zones; zones are listed hub first, then arm by arm in the order above, except that `019` lists its halls before its support wing (its section below).

- Implemented (S8.5): `HubArmsLayout(hubWidth, armWidth, armLengths)` takes one length per arm, so the support wing of `019` can be shorter than its halls. A family with *n* arms uses the first *n* sides in the order east, north, west, south: two arms (`018`) leave the hub east and north (an L), three leave its south side free (a T), four make a cross. Each `HubArm` has its own frame: *along* runs from the root on the hub's side to the tip, *across* from the arm's clockwise side (the right-hand side looking out from the hub: south for the east arm, east for the north arm, north for the west arm, west for the south arm) to its counter-clockwise side. `Piece(along₀, along₁, across₀, across₁)` is a rectangle of that frame, and pieces with the same along or across values share their edges exactly, so the families subdivide the arms without geometry of their own. `HubWithStrips(across₀, across₁, length)` unites the hub with one strip of every arm into one polygon (the mall of `014`). The footprint and that polygon are walked counter-clockwise around the hub, start at their lowest, then leftmost, vertex, and have no repeated or collinear vertices, so an arm as wide as the hub continues the hub's sides without a vertex at the hub's corner. `CountErrors` and `WidthErrors` give every family the same messages, for example `LobeCount must be at most 4 (lobes leave the core only on its four sides, D-100), got 5.` and `LobeWidth 13 m is wider than CoreWidth 12 m; an arm must not be wider than the hub it leaves, or it would overlap its neighbour beyond the hub's corner.`; a fifth arm is an `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` of the layout, which the families' validation never lets through. The layout's own tests reach it through `InternalsVisibleTo` from `Lod.Generators` to `Lod.Generators.Tests`.
```

```markdown
- Not modelled: individual bedrooms (D-097), the care station as a zone of its own, gardens.
- Implemented (S8.5): as specified; two wings leave the hub east and north. Zones `HUB` (*Communal Hub*, 256 m²), then per wing `W{i}-CO` (*Wing i Corridor*, 72 m²), `W{i}-B1`, `W{i}-B2` (*Wing i Bedrooms 1*, *Bedrooms 2*, 180 m² each); 1552 m² by default. The 14.4 m wings on the 16 m hub leave six 0.8 m hub corners as outdoor walls, which get no window (ADR-011, info `WindowOmitted`), so the default plan has 16 windows (217.92 m² of glazing): one on the hub's 16 m south side, two per bedroom zone (its side and its end), and one at each corridor's 2.4 m end. Every corridor adjoins the hub over 2.4 m, every bedroom zone the hub over 6 m and its corridor over 30 m. *Semantic Merge* keeps every zone, since no two zones of one type touch; *Perimeter Core* gives each hub corner a perimeter zone of its own, 0.8 m × 4.57 m (3.656 m²), and 9 m wings (3.5 m bedrooms and a 2 m corridor) fail it with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087). The short corners exposed a rounding fault of *Perimeter Core* on rotated plans, fixed in S8.5 ([below](#pipeline-consequences-of-s85)).
```

7. Replace

```markdown
- Not modelled: individual changing rooms and stores (D-097), spectator galleries, halls of different sizes, and different storey heights for halls and foyer.
```

   with

```markdown
- Not modelled: individual changing rooms and stores (D-097), spectator galleries, halls of different sizes, and different storey heights for halls and foyer.
- Implemented (S8.5): as specified; the halls are listed before the support wing, as the zone list above says, although the support wing is the second arm. Zones `FO` (*Foyer*, 400 m²), `H1`, `H2` (*Hall 1*, *Hall 2*, 600 m² each), `SV` (*Support*, 200 m²); 1800 m² by default, a T whose short stem is the support wing, with 10 windows (276 m² of glazing): the foyer's 20 m south entrance façade (WWR 0.5), three per hall, and three on the support wing. Every hall and the support wing adjoins the foyer over the hall width and no other zone. Three halls (2400 m²) close the foyer's south side, so the foyer has no outdoor wall; a foyer wider than the halls leaves its corners outdoor (a 24 m foyer with three halls: eight 2 m walls, each with a window). *Semantic Merge* keeps every zone; *Perimeter Core* gives `P-North-1` to `P-North-3`, `P-East-1`, `P-East-2`, `P-South`, `P-West-1`, `P-West-2`, and `CORE`.

#### Pipeline consequences of S8.5

Every plan simplifier and floor aggregator was run on the four families, canonical, at the two rotated placements of the test suite, and with the fewest and the most arms, and, while building the reference, in a sweep of 50 rotations from 0° to 353.5° with translations for each family with its fewest, default, and most arms, 11 m lobes on a 12 m core, and a 24 m foyer with three halls. It found one fault, now fixed:

- *Perimeter Core* united the trapezoids of one orientation bin with a plain union, which rounds every vertex to the clipping grid. On a rotated plan the wall on a footprint edge shorter than about 1 m, such as a 0.8 m hub corner of the care hub, then differs in direction from the source wall by more than the angular tolerance (ADR-004), so *Single Zone per Floor Type* and *Single Zone Building* reported `FacadeNotCovered` for it: in 22, 10, and 26 of 800 combinations of the care hub with 3, 2, and 4 wings in the sweep, and at the test suite's 131.4° placement. The union now keeps the footprint's own vertices; the mitred offset vertices keep their rounding, so every existing result is unchanged ([ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md), [ADR-002](ADR-002-geometry.md)). No tolerance or check was relaxed, and after the fix the sweep has no failure.

Nothing else in `Lod.Core` changed. Arms as wide as the hub give the footprint only re-entrant corners, which the bisector rule of *Perimeter Core* handles (S8.2); arms narrower than the hub add short steps at the hub's corners, which it handles too, each step getting a small perimeter zone. Arms not wider than twice the perimeter depth fail *Perimeter Core* with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087), a precondition of the simplifier that the generators do not impose.
```

- The hub-and-arms families share one layout (`HubArmsLayout`, S8.5); arms narrower than the hub leave short hub façades at its corners, which get no window below the ADR-011 limits (the care hub's 0.8 m corners by default).
```

```markdown
### S8.5 checklist — branching plans on a hub and its arms (`v0.8.4`)

S8.5 adds four plan generator components in *2 Generate* for the families that branch from a hub on the shared hub-and-arms layout ([ADR-006](../decisions/ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md)): the radial lobes (`SYN-TYP-010`), the branching mall (`SYN-TYP-014`), the care hub (`SYN-TYP-018`), and the foyer with halls (`SYN-TYP-019`). *Perimeter Core* keeps the footprint's own vertices in its perimeter zones ([ADR-008](../decisions/ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)). No existing component, input, output, or GUID changes.

#### Toolbar and icons

- [ ] *2 Generate* shows, after *Cafeteria*, *Radial Lobes* (a cross of lobes around a dark core), *Branching Mall* (a T of shop wings along a dark branching mall), *Care Hub* (a hub with a table and three narrower wings with dark corridors), and *Foyer Halls* (a white foyer between two halls, a dark support wing above, an entrance arrow below), each with its own green icon.
- [ ] *Radial Lobes* (RLobes) inputs, in order, with their defaults: Lobe Count 4, Lobe Length 10, Lobe Width 12, Core Width 12, Floor Height 3, Orientation 0, Dwelling Unit, Stair, Preview Location; one output, *Plan*.
- [ ] *Branching Mall* (BMall): Branch Count 3, Branch Length 40, Mall Width 8, Shop Depth 12, Anchor Depth 30, Floor Height 5, Orientation 0, Mall, Retail, Preview Location.
- [ ] *Care Hub* (CHub): Wing Count 3, Wing Length 30, Bedroom Depth 6, Corridor Width 2.4, Hub Width 16, Floor Height 3, Orientation 0, Care Bedroom, Care Communal, Corridor, Preview Location.
- [ ] *Foyer Halls* (FHalls): Hall Count 2, Hall Length 30, Hall Width 20, Support Length 10, Foyer Width 20, Floor Height 6, Orientation 0, Lobby, Activity Hall, Service, Preview Location.
- [ ] The count inputs are integers. With a preset input unconnected, each generator shows Grasshopper's missing-input warning and outputs nothing (D-065); a *Retail Preset* on the *Mall* input of *Branching Mall* gives `Error PresetSpaceTypeMismatch`.

#### Default plans

1. Place *Radial Lobes* with *Dwelling Unit Preset* and *Stair Preset* connected; connect *Inspect* to its *Plan*.
   - [ ] No runtime message; a cross of 624 m²: the core `ST` at x = y = 10–22 m and four lobes `L1` (east) to `L4` (south), each split along its axis into `L{i}-U1` and `L{i}-U2` of 60 m².
   - [ ] 16 windows, 115.2 m² of glazing (28.8 m² per orientation); the core has no outdoor wall.
   - [ ] The *Inspect* report equals `tests/Lod.Generators.Tests/RadialLobes/Snapshots/radial-lobes-canonical.txt`.
2. Place *Branching Mall* with *Mall Preset* and *Retail Preset* connected; connect *Inspect*.
   - [ ] No runtime message; a T of 7744 m²; `MALL` (1984 m²) is one polygon through the court at x = 70–102 m, y = 0–32 m and the three 8 m strips; `B1-S1`, `B1-S2`, `B1-AN` to `B3-AN` around it.
   - [ ] 16 windows, 806 m² of glazing; the mall's only window is on the court's south side.
   - [ ] The *Inspect* report equals `tests/Lod.Generators.Tests/BranchingMall/Snapshots/branching-mall-canonical.txt`.
3. Place *Care Hub* with *Care Bedroom Preset*, *Care Communal Preset*, and *Corridor Preset* connected; connect *Inspect*.
   - [ ] Six remarks `WindowOmitted`, one per 0.8 m hub corner; a T of 1552 m²: `HUB` at x = 30–46 m, y = 0–16 m and three wings, each `W{i}-CO` between `W{i}-B1` and `W{i}-B2`.
   - [ ] 16 windows, 217.92 m² of glazing; every corridor has one window at its end.
   - [ ] The *Inspect* report equals `tests/Lod.Generators.Tests/CareHub/Snapshots/care-hub-canonical.txt`.
4. Place *Foyer Halls* with *Lobby Preset*, *Activity Hall Preset*, and *Service Preset* connected; connect *Inspect*.
   - [ ] No runtime message; a T of 1800 m²: `FO` at x = 30–50 m, y = 0–20 m, `H1` east, `H2` west, `SV` north; zones listed `FO`, `H1`, `H2`, `SV`.
   - [ ] 10 windows, 276 m² of glazing; the foyer's one window is on its south entrance façade.
   - [ ] The *Inspect* report equals `tests/Lod.Generators.Tests/FoyerHalls/Snapshots/foyer-halls-canonical.txt`.

#### Combinations

5. Connect each plan to the four plan simplifiers (*Perimeter Core* Depth 4.57) and each floor three times, Multipliers 1, 3, 1, to the four floor aggregators.
   - [ ] Radial lobes: *Semantic Merge* gives the core and four dwelling zones, one per lobe; *Perimeter Core* gives `P-North-1` to `P-West-3` and a cross-shaped `CORE` of 122.5796 m².
   - [ ] Branching mall: *Semantic Merge* gives `MALL` and one `Retail` zone of 1920 m² per branch.
   - [ ] Care hub: *Semantic Merge* keeps all ten zones; *Perimeter Core* gives six small perimeter zones of 3.656 m² at the hub corners.
   - [ ] For all 16 combinations of each plan, *Validate* gives Passed = True (only `ConditionedFloorArea` may read `note`), no aggregator shows a warning, and *Convert2BEM* with Override = False converts without an error; every zone Brep has a positive volume (D-091).

#### Counts and rotated placement

6. Set each count to its smallest and largest value and repeat step 5 for each: Lobe Count 3 and 4, Branch Count 3 and 4, Wing Count 2 and 4, Hall Count 2 and 3.
   - [ ] Lobe Count 3: the core's south wall has a window; Branch Count 4: the mall has no outdoor wall; Wing Count 2: the wings leave the hub east and north; Hall Count 3: hall 3 is south of the foyer, which has no outdoor wall, and the zones are `FO`, `H1`, `H2`, `H3`, `SV`.
   - [ ] *Validate* gives Passed = True for every combination.
7. Put each default plan through *Transform Plan* with a 30° rotation and a move by 5.3, −2.1, 0, then through each simplifier and aggregator (Multipliers 1, 3, 1); repeat with 131.4° and 15.08, −153.9, 0.
   - [ ] *Validate* gives Passed = True for all 16 combinations of each placement, with the same total glazing as the unrotated plan; *Care Hub* through *Perimeter Core* and *Single Zone Building* at 131.4° passes (it failed with `FacadeNotCovered` before S8.5).

#### Parameters

   - [ ] *Radial Lobes* Lobe Count 5: `Error InvalidParameter: LobeCount must be at most 4 (lobes leave the core only on its four sides, D-100), got 5.`; Lobe Count 2: `Error InvalidParameter: LobeCount must be at least 3 (two lobes would be a bar through the core), got 2.`
   - [ ] *Radial Lobes* Lobe Width 13: `Error InvalidParameter: LobeWidth 13 m is wider than CoreWidth 12 m; an arm must not be wider than the hub it leaves, or it would overlap its neighbour beyond the hub's corner.`

   - [ ] *Foyer Halls* Hall Count 4: `Error InvalidParameter: HallCount must be at most 3 (the support wing takes one of the foyer's four sides, D-100), got 4.`
   - [ ] *Radial Lobes* Lobe Width 9 through *Perimeter Core* (Depth 4.57): `Error PlanTooNarrow: Perimeter depth 4.57 m leaves no valid core for this footprint: every wing and every part of the plan must be wider than twice the depth (9.14 m). Use a smaller depth or another simplifier.` and no floor; the other three simplifiers still work.
```

Items that a headless session cannot observe stay open for a person (D-056, D-058): the toolbar and canvas icons, the viewport previews (the cross, the T, the mall through its branches, the zone colours), the *Inspect* reports compared with the canonical snapshots (the generator tests check them), the zone IDs of *Semantic Merge* and *Perimeter Core* (the integration tests check them), the glazing values of *Validate*, and *Convert2BEM* with Override = False on each of the combinations. No example definition is part of S8.5; the pending S4 definition (`examples/grasshopper/checkpoint-1-pipeline.gh`) stays person-only (D-058).

If Step 1 printed a difference, or a person or a repeated session finds a failure, the stage stops: fix it on this branch with a test (core) or a build plus smoke test (Grasshopper) before continuing. Task 11 records this result in the guide and in the close-out commit, following the guide's "Recording the result".

Checkpoint 1 (D-030) is reached: stages S0–S4 of the [implementation roadmap](docs/plans/2026-09-30-implementation-roadmap.md) are complete (tag `v0.4.0`), and so are the revision stages S4.1 (tag `v0.4.1`, D-064) and S4.2 (tag `v0.4.2`, D-073), stage S5 (tag `v0.5.0`, D-079), and sub-stages S8.1 (tag `v0.8.0`), S8.2 (tag `v0.8.1`), S8.3 (tag `v0.8.2`, D-084), S8.4 (tag `v0.8.3`, D-101), and S8.5 (tag `v0.8.4`, D-103). The pipeline runs end to end for fifteen plan generators: program presets (the residential `ExampleResidentialPresets` and, for the non-residential departments of [ADR-014](docs/decisions/ADR-014-program-types-and-department-zoning.md), `ExampleNonResidentialPresets`, one typed preset and default preset component per space type) → a plan generator (one `PlanGenerator` subclass per typology family with typed parameter and preset records, [ADR-006](docs/decisions/ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md); `SYN-TYP-001` to `009` are the *High-Density Housing* families `HDHM-TYP-001` to `009`, D-099: `LinearPlanGenerator`, the double-sided internal corridor bar `SYN-TYP-009`; the bar families `StairBayBarGenerator` 001, `StairPairGenerator` 008, `GalleryBarGenerator` 002 with its gallery not modelled, and `TerraceRowGenerator` 006 with one dwelling zone per plot and storey, D-085; the non-rectangular families `PointPlateGenerator` 007, a square plate whose core has no outdoor wall, `WingedBandGenerator` 003, a comb, and `OpenCourtGenerator` 004, a U; `EnclosedCourtGenerator` 005, four wings around a court closed on all four sides, the first footprint with a hole; and the non-residential `OfficePlateGenerator` `SYN-TYP-017`, whose office work area is one ring-shaped zone around a central `Core` zone, `CafeteriaGenerator` `SYN-TYP-016`, a `Kitchen` zone along the whole north edge of a larger `Dining` zone; and the hub-and-arms families on the shared `HubArmsLayout`, a square hub with up to four arms (D-100, D-102): `RadialLobesGenerator` 010, `BranchingMallGenerator` 014, `CareHubGenerator` 018, and `FoyerHallsGenerator` 019; the non-residential ones zoned one zone per department, D-097; one typed preset per space type, D-061; optionally rotated and moved by `PlanTransform`, D-070) → plan simplifiers (`NoSimplification`, `SemanticMerge`, `PerimeterCore`, `SingleZonePerFloor`) → floor aggregators (`Stack`, `StackedFloorZoneMultiplier`, `SingleZonePerFloorType`, `SingleZoneBuilding`, D-071; floors and ceilings designated on the assembled stack, D-068) → validation → `Convert2BEM` (heat transfer between zones an export option, off by default, D-069), as described in [docs/architecture/pipeline.md](docs/architecture/pipeline.md). Every generator places one centred window per outdoor wall (D-026), except on a wall shorter than 1 m or where the window would be narrower or lower than 0.3 m (`GeometryLimits`, info `WindowOmitted`, [ADR-011](docs/decisions/ADR-011-minimum-wall-and-window-geometry.md)). Wherever zones are merged and walls are rebuilt (`SemanticMerge`, `PerimeterCore`, `SingleZonePerFloor`, `SingleZonePerFloorType`, `SingleZoneBuilding`), every outdoor wall carries one centred window with the glazed area of the source windows it covers (`CentredWindows`, D-079, [ADR-012](docs/decisions/ADR-012-centred-windows.md)); the other steps keep their input's windows. Merged zones are unions of their sources as tiles that keep the sources' own vertices, so they may have holes and stay exact on rotated plans, and vertices on edges are recognised at their original positions (D-088, [ADR-002](docs/decisions/ADR-002-geometry.md)). Every step supports footprints with holes: a court is outside the building, its edges are outdoor walls facing into it, every footprint edge, court edges included, must be covered by outdoor zone edges (ADR-002), and `PerimeterCore` gives the court façades their own zones `P-Court-<Bin>` around a core that is a ring ([ADR-008](docs/decisions/ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md)); `PerimeterCore` needs every part of a footprint, including the wings between the outer and the court façades, to be wider than twice its depth and otherwise fails with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087). Its perimeter zones keep the footprint's own vertices, so short façades stay covered on rotated plans (ADR-008, S8.5). Vertical aggregation follows [ADR-013](docs/decisions/ADR-013-vertical-aggregation-methods.md), which supersedes [ADR-009](docs/decisions/ADR-009-vertical-aggregation.md) where it differs. In *Convert2BEM* and the previews, zone solids are outward-oriented and every surface and window faces out of its zone (D-091). ADR-006, ADR-011, ADR-012, ADR-013, ADR-014, and the S4.2, S5, S8.1, S8.2, S8.3, S8.4, and S8.5 plans were accepted by the controller and await the owner's reading (D-075, D-080, D-084, D-098). In Grasshopper every component has an icon drawn by `scripts/make_icons.py` (D-063), and the generator, *Transform Plan*, simplifier, and aggregator components have a *Preview Location* input that moves only their preview (D-062). Until the owner says otherwise, changes need not keep backward compatibility, but component GUIDs never change (D-064).

The precedent set is the four-source family set `SYN-TYP-001` to `SYN-TYP-019` of every program type, housing and non-residential ([docs/research/precedents/syn-typologies.md](docs/research/precedents/syn-typologies.md), D-093, D-094); `SYN-TYP-013` is set aside (D-096), non-residential floors are zoned one zone per department (D-097, [ADR-014](docs/decisions/ADR-014-program-types-and-department-zoning.md)), and BEMGen documents use the `SYN-TYP-*` IDs (D-099). The families `017` and `016` were built in S8.4 and `010`, `014`, `018`, `019` in S8.5, the hub-and-arms families with at most four arms because the shared layout is axis-aligned (D-100); `015`, `011` (S8.6) and `012` (S8.7) are specified in ADR-006 and built before S6 (D-098).

Not implemented yet: the four-source families of S8.6–S8.7, `Lod.Export` and Convert2IDF (S6), and Grasshopper UX and release (S9). The precedent study of S7 was carried out in a separate repository (D-082); its nine families are paraphrased in [docs/research/precedents/hdhm-typologies.md](docs/research/precedents/hdhm-typologies.md). A zone only about 1 m wide on a rotated plan can fail validation after a merging simplifier, a known limit of clipping on the grid (D-090). The example program presets hold illustrative values, not DOE prototype values, and the `Convert2BEM` output layout stays provisional until a ClimateStudio reference definition exists (D-023).
```

Checkpoint 1 reached: stages S0–S4 of the [implementation roadmap](docs/plans/2026-09-30-implementation-roadmap.md) are complete (tag `v0.4.0`), followed by the revision stages S4.1 (tag `v0.4.1`) and S4.2 (tag `v0.4.2`), stage S5 (tag `v0.5.0`), and sub-stages S8.1 (tag `v0.8.0`), S8.2 (tag `v0.8.1`), S8.3 (tag `v0.8.2`), S8.4 (tag `v0.8.3`), and S8.5 (tag `v0.8.4`). In Rhino 8 the plugin runs the whole pipeline for fifteen plan generators: program presets with conditioning (a default preset component per space type, housing and non-residential); the linear plan generator and the eight other typologies of the *High-Density Housing* precedent study (`SYN-TYP-001`–`009`, ADR-006), the bars *Stair Bay Bar*, *Stair Pair*, *Gallery Bar*, and *Terrace Row*, the non-rectangular *Point Plate*, *Winged Band*, and *Open Court*, *Enclosed Court*, whose footprint has the court as a hole, the non-residential *Office Plate* (`SYN-TYP-017`, a work-area ring around a central core) and *Cafeteria* (`SYN-TYP-016`, a kitchen and servery edge beside a dining field), and the branching plans on one hub-and-arms layout, *Radial Lobes* (`SYN-TYP-010`), *Branching Mall* (`SYN-TYP-014`), *Care Hub* (`SYN-TYP-018`), and *Foyer Halls* (`SYN-TYP-019`), the non-residential ones zoned one zone per department (D-097, ADR-014), each with one preset input per space type and explicit windows (none on a wall shorter than 1 m or for a window below 0.3 m, ADR-011); *Transform Plan* to rotate and move a plan; four plan simplifiers (Z0–Z3); four floor aggregators (Stack, StackedFloorZoneMultiplier, SingleZonePerFloorType, SingleZoneBuilding) that designate floors and ceilings on the assembled stack and warn about unsupported storeys; invariant validation; and `Convert2BEM`, which writes heat transfer between zones only when asked (off by default). Wherever zones are merged and walls are rebuilt, every outdoor wall carries one centred window with the glazed area of the windows it covers, so glazing is conserved per wall, façade, orientation, and building (D-079); merged zones stay exact on rotated plans (D-088); every simplifier and aggregator supports footprints with holes, with the court façades as outdoor walls; and zone solids, surfaces, and windows are oriented out of their zones (D-091). Every component has an icon, and every component with a viewport preview has a *Preview Location* input that moves only its preview. The pipeline is described in [docs/architecture/pipeline.md](docs/architecture/pipeline.md); the example definition `examples/grasshopper/checkpoint-1-pipeline.gh`, which wires every simplifier into every aggregator, is still to be built by a person. Next: S8.6–S8.7 (the remaining four-source families, D-098), then Convert2IDF (S6) and Grasshopper UX and release (S9). The `Convert2BEM` output stays provisional until a ClimateStudio reference definition exists (D-023), and the example program presets hold illustrative values, not DOE prototype values.
```

#### Result (S8.5)

Run by the controller as a scripted headless Grasshopper session (D-056) on 2026-10-02, on the verified reference of this sub-stage (`12a7e8318bb6f54dcffd59636ca2fe8e11113d6d`, whose `src/`, `tests/`, and `scripts/` are identical to the executed branch): Rhino 8.25.25314.11001, .NET 8.0.31, `BEMGen.gha` built in Release with 0 warnings, *BEMGen Info* version `0.8.3+12a7e8318bb6f54dcffd59636ca2fe8e11113d6d` (the version becomes 0.8.4 at the close-out); 54 scenarios.

- Passed: *Radial Lobes* (RLobes), *Branching Mall* (BMall), *Care Hub* (CHub), and *Foyer Halls* (FHalls) in *2 Generate*, all primary, with the GUIDs of the S8.5 plan and 24 × 24 icons; the integer count input first, the presets in record order, *Preview Location* last; unconnected presets give Grasshopper's missing-input warnings.
- Passed: *Radial Lobes* defaults: box (0, 0, 0)–(32, 32, 3), footprint 624 m²; *Semantic Merge* 5 zones, *Perimeter Core* 13, *Single Zone per Floor* 1; *Stack Floors* (one floor) boundaries Adiabatic 24, Ground 9, Outdoors 25. *Branching Mall*: footprint 7744 m², height 5 m; 4, 9, and 1 zones; Adiabatic 42, Ground 10, Outdoors 26. *Care Hub*: box (0, 0, 0)–(76, 46, 3), 1552 m², six `WindowOmitted` remarks for the 0.8 m hub corners; 10, 17, and 1 zones; Adiabatic 30, Ground 10, Outdoors 32. *Foyer Halls*: box (0, 0, 0)–(80, 30, 6), 1800 m²; 4, 9, and 1 zones; Adiabatic 6, Ground 4, Outdoors 14. The *Branching Mall* preview clipping box reports y = −0.72, because `PreviewGoo.ClippingBox` uses Rhino's fast, approximate box of the untrimmed planar surfaces of the mall zone; the geometry and the Breps are not affected.
- Passed: the 89 zone Breps inspected are valid solids, with no negative volume anywhere; *Single Zone Building* (1, 3, 1) previews one solid per family, (0, 0, 0)–(32, 32, 15), (172, 102, 25), (76, 46, 15), and (80, 30, 30), and *Validate* Passed = `True`.
- Passed: all 320 combinations (the four families on the canonical plan, rotated by 30° and moved by (5.3, −2.1, 0), rotated by 131.4° and moved by (15.08, −153.9, 0), and with the fewest and the most arms at 131.4°, each through the four simplifiers and the four aggregators with 1, 3, 1): *Validate* Passed = `True`, no error.
- Passed: the counts below and above their ranges (`Error InvalidParameter: LobeCount must be at least 3 (two lobes would be a bar through the core), got 2.`, `… at most 4 (lobes leave the core only on its four sides, D-100), got 5.`, and Branch Count 2 and 5, Wing Count 1 and 5, Hall Count 1 and 4 likewise); arms wider than the hub (`Error InvalidParameter: LobeWidth 14 m is wider than CoreWidth 12 m; an arm must not be wider than the hub it leaves, or it would overlap its neighbour beyond the hub's corner.`, the care hub's 14.4 m wings on a 12 m hub, the foyer's 25 m halls on a 20 m foyer); `Error InvalidParameter: BranchLength must be positive, got 0.`; 9 m lobes into *Perimeter Core*: `Error PlanTooNarrow: Perimeter depth 4.57 m leaves no valid core for this footprint: …`.
- Not performed, open for a person (D-058): the viewport look of the previews, the icons in the toolbar, the *Inspect* reports compared with the snapshots (the generator tests check them), and example definitions.

The log ends with D-101 when this plan is approved, so the entries are D-102 and D-103; if the log ends with another number, use the next free numbers, change the references to them (D-103 cites D-102; Tasks 1, 5, and 6 of this plan cite D-102 for the layout choices; AGENTS.md of Step 5 cites D-103) to match, and say so in the commit body.

```markdown
### D-102 — The layout choices of the hub-and-arms families

- **Date:** <date> · **Decided by:** controller (unsupervised run, D-098) · **Status:** Provisional
- Why: [ADR-006](ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md) fixes the arm order of the shared hub-and-arms layout (east, north, west, south) but not which sides a family with fewer than four arms uses, and its shared zone-order rule (hub first, then arm by arm) differs from the zone order of its section on `SYN-TYP-019`; the S8.5 reference settled both and made the layout's internals visible to their tests.
- A family with *n* arms uses the first *n* sides in the arm order, so two care-hub wings (`SYN-TYP-018`) leave the hub east and north, an L, not a bar through the hub; this is the only rule consistent with the foyer with halls (hall 1 east, the support wing north, hall 2 west, hall 3 south) and with three arms whose free hub side faces south. A straight bar for two arms would change only the side selection of `HubArmsLayout` and ADR-006.
- The foyer with halls (`SYN-TYP-019`) lists its zones `FO`, `H1…Hn`, `SV`, as its ADR-006 section says: its support wing comes after the halls, the one exception to the arm-by-arm order, which ADR-006's shared paragraph records.
- `Lod.Generators.csproj` makes its internals visible to `Lod.Generators.Tests` (`InternalsVisibleTo`), the first use of the item in the repository, so the internal `HubArmsLayout` is tested directly and not only through its four generators.
```

```markdown
### D-103 — S8.5 complete

- **Date:** <date> · **Decided by:** controller (unsupervised run, D-098) · **Status:** Provisional
- Sub-stage S8.5 (D-098) is complete and tagged `v0.8.4`; after its gate and a fresh clone of the tag pass `scripts/verify.ps1`, `main` and `v0.8.4` are pushed (D-098). The four families that branch from a hub are implemented as [ADR-006](ADR-006-plan-generation-mechanism.md) specifies them on one shared, axis-aligned layout, `HubArmsLayout` (internal, `Lod.Generators/HubArms/`): a square hub with up to four arms of one width centred on its sides in the order east, north, west, south, a family with *n* arms using the first *n* sides (D-102), each arm with its own frame in which the families cut their zones; an arm wider than the hub and more than four arms are `InvalidParameter` (D-100). New generators and components in *2 Generate*, with icons: *Radial Lobes* (`RadialLobesGenerator`, `SYN-TYP-010`, RLobes, `a9f132c2-40c3-40b9-ba6a-3f3e1f2ced80`): 3–4 lobes of two dwellings each around a `Stair` core; *Branching Mall* (`BranchingMallGenerator`, `SYN-TYP-014`, BMall, `d886b33f-7b51-41f9-b160-a550e6aadd2b`): one `Mall` zone through a central court and 3–4 branches of `Retail` shop wings and anchors; *Care Hub* (`CareHubGenerator`, `SYN-TYP-018`, CHub, `7d3d1002-c695-47df-afd9-48f50c5b3e90`): 2–4 wings of a `Corridor` between two `CareBedroom` zones around a `CareCommunal` hub, whose 0.8 m corners beside the default wings get no window (ADR-011); *Foyer Halls* (`FoyerHallsGenerator`, `SYN-TYP-019`, FHalls, `6638efe7-c0e8-45b6-89b1-1e9e3f725c15`): 2–3 `ActivityHall` zones and a `Service` wing around a `Lobby` foyer, zones `FO`, `H1…Hn`, `SV` (D-102). No record or default of ADR-006 changed; ADR-006 records the details settled in S8.5.
- *Perimeter Core* keeps the footprint's own vertices in its perimeter zones: it united each orientation bin's trapezoids with a plain union, whose grid rounding turned a footprint edge shorter than about 1 m (the care hub's corners) by more than the angular tolerance on rotated plans, so the single-zone aggregators reported `FacadeNotCovered` (up to 26 of 800 combinations in a sweep, and at the test suite's 131.4° placement). Fixed test-first and documented in [ADR-008](ADR-008-perimeter-core-corners.md) and [ADR-002](ADR-002-geometry.md); no tolerance or check was relaxed, and every existing result is unchanged.
- The eighteen existing snapshots are byte-identical; four canonical snapshots are new. 193 core, 360 generator, and 1184 integration tests pass on `net8.0`, including every family with every simplifier and aggregator on the canonical plan, rotated by 30° and moved by (5.3, −2.1) m and rotated by 131.4° and moved by (15.08, −153.9) m, and with its fewest and most arms; arms not wider than twice the perimeter depth fail *Perimeter Core* with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087).
- Smoke test (D-056): the controller's scripted headless Grasshopper session on the verified reference (`12a7e83`, whose `src/`, `tests/`, and `scripts/` equal the executed branch) in Rhino 8.25.25314.11001 (.NET 8.0.31) passed every performed item: the 4 generator components registered with their GUIDs, integer count inputs, presets in record order, *Preview Location* last, and 24 × 24 icons; the default radial lobes (624 m²), branching mall (7744 m²), care hub (1552 m², six `WindowOmitted` corners), and foyer with halls (1800 m²) with every zone a valid solid; all 320 combinations (four families, five placements and counts, four simplifiers, four aggregators, 1/3/1) with *Validate* passing and no negative volume; and the error cases. It is recorded in `docs/development/grasshopper-smoke-test.md` and the close-out commit; the viewport look, the icons in the toolbar, and example definitions stay open for a person (D-058).
- S8.6 (families 015 and 011) can start.

### Task 12: Fresh-clone gate and push (controller, D-098)

**Files:** none

D-098 pushes each sub-stage after its gate: the controller performs this task itself (a push is never delegated), and never with force. Its steps stay unticked in the plan, because they run after the close-out commit.

- [ ] **Step 1: Check the state to push**

```bash
git status --short
git fetch origin
git rev-parse main v0.8.4^{commit}
git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main main && echo fast-forward
git tag --list "v0.8.*"
git ls-remote --tags origin "v0.8.*"
```

Expected: no output from `git status --short`; the two SHAs are equal (the tag points at the tip of `main`); `fast-forward` (local `main` contains `origin/main`, which was at `49efa75` when this plan was written); the tags `v0.8.0` to `v0.8.4`; `ls-remote` shows which of them the remote has. If `origin/main` has commits that local `main` does not, or the remote already has a `v0.8.4` that points at another commit, stop and report: nothing is pushed, and no rebase or force push is attempted. If the remote lacks one of `v0.8.0` to `v0.8.3`, report it to the person; this task pushes only `main` and `v0.8.4`.

- [ ] **Step 2: Verify a fresh clone of the tag**

```bash
git clone --branch v0.8.4 . ../BEMGen-v0.8.4-clone
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ../BEMGen-v0.8.4-clone/scripts/verify.ps1
git -C ../BEMGen-v0.8.4-clone describe --tags
```

Expected: the clone reports a detached `HEAD` at `v0.8.4`; verify builds the clone with `0 Warning(s)` and `0 Error(s)` and ends with `VERIFY PASSED`, with 193 core, 360 generators, and 1184 integration tests passed on net8.0; `describe` prints `v0.8.4`. Then delete the clone (`rm -rf ../BEMGen-v0.8.4-clone` in Git Bash). If verify fails, stop: nothing is pushed.

- [ ] **Step 3: Push (no force)**

```bash
git push origin main
git push origin v0.8.4
git ls-remote --tags origin "v0.8.4*"
git status -sb
```

Expected: each push succeeds without `--force` (`main` as a fast-forward, the tag as `[new tag]`); `ls-remote` lists `v0.8.4` with its peeled `^{}` line; `git status -sb` shows `## main...origin/main` with nothing ahead or behind. A rejected push is not retried with force: stop and report.

## Exit criteria

- `HubArmsLayout` lays out a square hub with up to four arms of one width in the order east, north, west, south, with arm frames whose pieces share their edges exactly and a counter-clockwise footprint at the origin; it is tested directly by `HubArmsLayoutTests` (27 tests), which failed to build on the code before it.
- `RadialLobesGenerator` (010), `BranchingMallGenerator` (014), `CareHubGenerator` (018), and `FoyerHallsGenerator` (019) implement ADR-006 with their defaults, zone IDs and order, and validation (3–4 lobes, 3–4 branches, 2–4 wings, 2–3 halls, D-100; no arm wider than the hub); each has tiling, adjacency, window, count, invalid-parameter, preset, determinism, provenance, and snapshot tests that failed to build on the code before them.
- *Perimeter Core* keeps the footprint's own vertices in its perimeter zones, tested by `PerimeterCoreShortEdgeTests` (150 cases), 58 of which failed on the code before it; every existing result is unchanged; ADR-008 and ADR-002 record the rule.
- Every family validates with every plan simplifier and floor aggregator, on its canonical plan, at two rotated and moved placements, and with its fewest and most arms (82 cases each); 9 m lobes and 9 m care wings fail *Perimeter Core* with `PlanTooNarrow` (D-087).
- Four generator components in *2 Generate*, with the GUIDs of this plan, their defaults, integer count inputs, required preset inputs, *Preview Location* last, and icons (57 icons, the existing ones byte-identical); no existing component, input, or GUID changed.
- Snapshots: the eighteen existing snapshots byte-identical; four new canonical snapshots.
- ADR-006, `pipeline.md`, `syn-typologies.md`, and `program-presets.md` describe S8.5; the S8.5 smoke-test checklist and its headless result are in the guide.
- `scripts/verify.ps1` prints `VERIFY PASSED`; tests: 193 core, 360 generators, 1184 integration on `net8.0`; 0 warnings.
- Version 0.8.4, tag `v0.8.4`; AGENTS.md, README, the roadmap Progress line and detailed-plans row, and the decision-log entries D-102 and D-103 are written; this plan is ticked and marked Done.
- After a fresh clone of `v0.8.4` passes `scripts/verify.ps1`, `main` and `v0.8.4` are pushed without force (D-098).

## Notes for the reviewer

1. **Source.** Every code block, snapshot, and documentation replacement of Tasks 1 to 9 was extracted programmatically from a verified reference implementation built on `main` at `49efa75`, 9 commits from `184fbb9` to `12a7e83`, each passing `scripts/verify.ps1` with 0 warnings; the tasks keep the reference's commit order, one task per commit (no reordering was needed: a relative-link check of every reference commit found no link to a file that a later commit creates). The only content not taken from the reference is this plan's own commit and the close-out of Task 11 (adapted from the reference's proposed texts, with the decision numbers of note 13). Every task was then replayed on a worktree of `main` at `49efa75` (its branch standing in for `main`; nothing fetched, pulled, tagged, or pushed), taking each file content and each replacement from this plan's text: the red build errors of Tasks 1, 2, 3, 5, and 6 (with line and column numbers and in the compiler's order), the 58 failing cases of Task 4 and their messages, the single failing snapshot test of Tasks 2, 3, 5, and 6 and the received snapshots (byte-identical to the expected content), the failure of `make_icons.py` before Task 8 and the 57 hashes after it (every PNG byte-identical to the reference's), the counts after every task, a 0-warning build after every task, every documentation replacement (each "Replace" text occurs exactly once when it is applied), the link checks, the close-out edits, and `VERIFY PASSED` on every merge are observed results. The replay's end state equals the reference: `git diff --stat ref/s8.5 replay/s8.5` limited to the 34 paths the reference changes prints nothing for the state before the close-out, and after it lists only `docs/development/grasshopper-smoke-test.md`, whose difference is the result section of Task 11, Step 7; `Directory.Build.props` (0.8.4), AGENTS.md, README.md, the decision log, the roadmap, and this plan are files the reference does not change. Every observation of a first replay was observed again, unchanged, in a second replay of the final plan from a fresh worktree.
2. **Slices and branches.** The roadmap names no slices for S8.5; this plan uses five slices and a close-out, named after D-004: `feature/hub-arms-lobes-mall` (Tasks 1–3), `fix/perimeter-core-footprint-vertices` (Task 4), `feature/care-hub-foyer-halls` (Tasks 5–7), `feature/hub-arms-components` (Task 8), `docs/architecture-hub-arms` (Tasks 9–10), and `chore/repo-s8.5-close-out`. Merges rebase onto local `main`. As in S8.4, the stage is pushed at its end (D-098): Task 12 is the controller's, after the tag and a fresh clone of it pass `scripts/verify.ps1`. `origin/main` was at `49efa75` when this plan was written, so the push is a fast-forward.
3. **Links.** A relative-link check (file targets and heading anchors, GitHub's slug rule) over every Markdown file of the replay found: no broken link at the plan commit or after any commit of the replay (checked after Tasks 3, 4, 7, 9, 10, and 11). Task 9 renames the heading `## Grasshopper components through S8.4` of `pipeline.md` to `… through S8.5` and updates its two links (in `pipeline.md` and `program-presets.md`) in the same commit.
4. **The baseline is 193 core, 224 generators, and 704 integration tests** (D-101).
5. **Tests that were not test-first, and why.** The integration matrices of Tasks 2, 3, 5, and 6 (328 cases) passed at once in the reference and in the replay: the surface builder, the four simplifiers, the four aggregators, and validation already handle the L, T, and cross footprints with their re-entrant corners, and the care hub's matrix passes because Task 4 comes first (note 6); each task says to stop if a case fails. The two tests of Task 7 check the existing *Perimeter Core* precondition (D-087) on the new families, so they pass at once by design. Task 8 is Grasshopper code, checked by the build, the embedded icons, and Task 10. The build-error red phases list only declaration-level errors: the compiler stops before binding method bodies.
6. **Why Task 4 comes before the care hub, and what it repairs.** The reference found the defect through the care hub: its integration matrix failed at the 131.4° placement for *Perimeter Core* × *Single Zone per Floor Type* and × *Single Zone Building* (`FacadeNotCovered` on a 0.8 m hub corner). The fix is tested on a plan of its own (a block with an 18.4 m wing on a 20 m side), so it can come first and the care hub's matrix passes at once. The replay confirmed it: with `Strategies.cs` of Task 3 put back after Task 5, `CareHubTypologyTests` reported `Failed!  - Failed:     2, Passed:    80, Skipped:     0, Total:    82`, the failing cases `EveryCombinationValidatesOnARotatedAndTranslatedPlan(simplifier: "PerimeterCore", aggregator: "SingleZonePerFloorType", placement: 1)` and `EveryCombinationValidatesOnARotatedAndTranslatedPlan(simplifier: "PerimeterCore", aggregator: "SingleZoneBuilding", placement: 1)`, each with `FacadeNotCovered` on `P-West-2/W2` of every storey (`Rebuilt façades cover 0 m of this 0.8 m wall (D-041).`); with Task 4 restored, all 82 passed again. An uncommitted sweep in the reference (50 rotations from 0° to 353.5° with translations, each through the 4 simplifiers × 4 aggregators with 1, 3, 1, 800 runs per plan) found 22, 10, and 26 failures for the care hub with 3, 2, and 4 wings before the fix and none after it, and none for the radial lobes (3 and 4 lobes, and 11 m lobes on a 12 m core), the branching mall (3 and 4), the care hub, the foyer with halls (2 and 3, and 3 halls on a 24 m foyer), and, as a regression sweep, the linear plan, the point plate, the winged band, the open court, the enclosed court, and the office plate; the sweep was not replayed. The reference first united the trapezoids with `PolygonOps.UnionTiles`, which also restores the offset vertices; `scripts/verify.ps1` then failed two snapshot tests (`AggregatorTests.MultiplierSnapshot` and `SimplifierTests.CanonicalSnapshots(PerimeterCore)`: the canonical linear plan's 45° corner wall `P-South/W4` flipped its `facing=` from North to East), so only footprint vertices are restored (ADR-008); no snapshot was accepted with a change.
7. **Layout choices of the reference (D-102).** ADR-006 fixes the arm order but not which sides fewer than four arms use; *n* arms take the first *n* sides, the only rule consistent with the foyer with halls (hall 1 east, the support wing north, hall 2 west, hall 3 south) and with the T whose free side faces south, so two care-hub wings form an L (east and north), not a bar (east and west). If the controller prefers east and west for two arms, only the side selection of `HubArmsLayout` and ADR-006 change. The foyer with halls lists `FO`, `H1…Hn`, `SV` as its own ADR-006 section says, not arm by arm (`FO`, `H1`, `SV`, `H2`, …) as the shared paragraph said; Task 9 adds the exception to the shared paragraph. `InternalsVisibleTo` is new in the repository (no project used it; `StairBayLayout` is tested only through its generators); the brief asked for direct layout tests, which need it. The close-out records the three as the provisional controller decision D-102.
8. **One width per plan.** All arms of a plan have one width (ADR-006 gives one `A` per family), and each arm its own length (the support wing of `019` is shorter than its halls). The care hub reports a wing wider than its hub through the shared width message, `The wing width 2·BedroomDepth + CorridorWidth … m is wider than HubWidth … m; …`, which states ADR-006's condition `2·BedroomDepth + CorridorWidth ≤ HubWidth` in the words of an arm. More than four arms reaching `HubArmsLayout` throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`: a programming error, unreachable through the families' validation (D-100).
9. **Short hub corners.** An arm narrower than the hub by a small amount leaves correspondingly short hub-corner walls, which get no window below 1 m (ADR-011); the tests cover 0.5 m (11 m lobes on a 12 m core, in the reference's sweep) and 0.8 m corners. Corners near the clipping grid may meet the known limit of D-090; they are not tested.
10. **Smoke-test values.** The values of Task 10 were observed in one scripted headless Rhino 8.25 session (D-056) on the reference's last commit `12a7e83`, which reported the informational version `0.8.3+12a7e83…`; they were not re-observed on the replay. Task 10, Step 1 checks that `src/`, `tests/`, and `scripts/` of the branch equal the reference's, so the record applies; the replay found no difference. The viewport, the toolbar icons, the *Inspect* texts beyond the footprint line, the zone IDs, the *Validate* glazing values, and *Convert2BEM* with Override = False were not observed.
11. **Icons.** The 53 existing PNGs are byte-identical after Task 8 (Pillow 12.3.0); the four new ones equal the reference's bytes. The contact-sheet description is from the replay's sheet.
12. **Clean working tree.** The `git status` expectations assume a working tree with no untracked files outside `.gitignore` before Task 1.
13. **D-numbers.** The reference's report proposed D-102 for the completion entry; this plan uses D-102 for the layout choices of note 7, which Tasks 1, 5, and 6 and the completion entry cite, and D-103 for the completion.
14. **`check_plans.py` false positives.** The Global Constraints line that states the no-attribution rule is flagged as a possible attribution; it states the rule. The `make_icons.py` docstring line that names the Pillow version of the icons is flagged, as in S4.1 to S8.4.
